========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I guess… mostly my sleep. It’s gotten all broken up, and I can’t seem to fix it. I work overnights, so you’d think when I get off I’d be exhausted and just knock out, but I’ll lie there wide awake, like my body forgot how to sleep. And then when I finally do drift off, I wake up scared, like something happened, and I don’t feel rested at all.

It’s been messing with me. I used to be able to sleep after a shift if the room was dark enough—my neighbor’s dog used to bark in the mornings and I’d still sleep through it sometimes—but now it’s like even quiet doesn’t help. That’s why I came in, I think. I just feel like I’m not really sleeping anymore.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… yeah, my mind does that a lot. Especially when I’m trying to lay down or even just sit quiet, it starts going over every little thing I think I messed up.

Like with patients, I’ll think, did I turn them right, did I miss something, did I answer too slow… and then it’ll jump to my family, like things I should’ve said different or done better. My aunt used to say if you’re caring for somebody, you don’t get to be careless, and I keep hearing stuff like that in my head.

And then it feels like… I don’t know. Like I’m not a good aide. Like I’m worthless at it. And sometimes it feels like God is mad at me because I didn’t protect people better, even when I can’t even name exactly what I was supposed to protect them from. It just keeps going around and around.

Most days it’s like I wake up already heavy, like there’s this empty feeling sitting on me before I even get out of bed.\n\nI used to go to church, and I used to do Zumba sometimes—my cousin kept telling me I had no rhythm, but I’d still go—and I helped with the kids at the center too. I don’t know… it feels like that version of me is just gone now. Like I open my eyes and she’s not there anymore.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how often you feel this way throughout the day? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d call them nightmares exactly… sometimes I don’t even remember what I was dreaming. It’s more like I finally fall asleep after my overnight shift, after just lying there staring at the wall, and then I wake up scared like something happened.

And it’s strange because I’ll be so tired, like my body should just shut off, but it doesn’t. I keep thinking maybe it’s the schedule, because nights mess everything up, you know, and then the sun’s coming up and people are mowing lawns or my neighbor’s dog starts barking, and then I’m just awake again.

But when I do drift off, I don’t feel rested. I wake up jumpy and scared, like I didn’t sleep at all. I can’t really say I’m reliving one specific thing, though.

My aunt used to say if you’re caring for somebody, you don’t get to be careless, and I keep hearing stuff like that in my head.\n\nAnd then it feels like… I don’t know. Like I’m not a good aide. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s more that my mind won’t slow down, I think. Like I get off the overnight shift and I should be exhausted, because everybody else is starting their day and I’m just getting home, and I’ll lie down but I’m just… awake. Wide awake.

Sometimes I start thinking about one thing, then another thing, and then I’m listening to noises outside, or thinking I forgot to do something, and then it’s already later than I meant it to be. And if I do finally drift off, it doesn’t feel like real sleep. I’ll wake up scared, like something happened, but I don’t always remember a dream or anything specific.

That’s kind of why I came in, I guess. My sleep is all broken up now. I wake up feeling like I never rested at all.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know… I mean, church is still church, you know? I grew up going, my aunt used to make sure we were dressed right and on time, even if somebody’s shoes were missing. So it’s not like I don’t care about it.

And the kids at the center… kids are funny, they’ll say anything. One little boy used to call me “Miss Snacks” because I always knew where the crackers were. I remember that.

But lately… I’m not sure if I’d say it doesn’t make me happy, exactly. I guess I haven’t really been paying attention to it like that. Sometimes I go through the motions and then afterward I’m thinking about whether I did everything right, or if people noticed I was quiet. So… I don’t know. It’s hard for me to tell.

Like from the minute I open my eyes, I already feel heavy and empty, like I’m carrying something before anything even happens.\n\nSometimes I’ll think about how I used to get up for church, or go to Zumba even when I didn’t feel like it—there was this one lady who always stood in the front like she was on TV—and I used to help with the kids at the center too. Now it’s like that person is just gone. So, yeah… it doesn’t really come and go much. It’s just kind of there.\n\nDoctor: Have you gone through any particularly stressful or traumatic experience recently that might be connected to your sleep problems or the way you feel when you wake up scared?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like one big thing that happened, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI mean, work is stressful, but it’s always kind of been that way. People needing things all night, call lights, the same hallway lights buzzing… there’s this one machine at work that makes this little clicking sound and it gets stuck in my head sometimes, but I don’t know if that counts.\n\nI just wake up scared and I can’t always tell why. It’s not like I can point to something and say, “That’s it.” I wish I could, because then maybe it would make more sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself thinking about a specific moment or event over and over again, or having sudden memories that feel very intense and upsetting, even if you can’t point to one big thing that happened?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really a specific moment, no. It’s not like a movie scene that keeps playing or anything.\n\nSometimes my mind gets stuck on little things, but not like… I don’t know, not one big memory. Like I’ll think about work stuff, or I’ll remember something small someone said, and then somehow I’m thinking about whether I left laundry in the washer, and then I’m thinking about how my aunt used to get mad if clothes smelled damp… but that’s not what you mean, right?\n\nI just wake up upset sometimes, but I can’t point to one thing that’s causing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find your mind going over the same thoughts or worries, even when you're trying to rest or do something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, my mind does that a lot. Especially when I’m trying to lay down or even just sit quiet, it starts going over every little thing I think I messed up.\n\nLike with patients, I’ll think, did I turn them right, did I miss something, did I answer too slow… and then it’ll jump to my family, like things I should’ve said different or done better. My aunt used to say if you’re caring for somebody, you don’t get to be careless, and I keep hearing stuff like that in my head.\n\nAnd then it feels like… I don’t know. Like I’m not a good aide. Like I’m worthless at it. And sometimes it feels like God is mad at me because I didn’t protect people better, even when I can’t even name exactly what I was supposed to protect them from. It just keeps going around and around.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having nightmares or vivid dreams that make you feel like you're reliving a stressful or upsetting experience?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call them nightmares exactly… sometimes I don’t even remember what I was dreaming. It’s more like I finally fall asleep after my overnight shift, after just lying there staring at the wall, and then I wake up scared like something happened.\n\nAnd it’s strange because I’ll be so tired, like my body should just shut off, but it doesn’t. I keep thinking maybe it’s the schedule, because nights mess everything up, you know, and then the sun’s coming up and people are mowing lawns or my neighbor’s dog starts barking, and then I’m just awake again.\n\nBut when I do drift off, I don’t feel rested. I wake up jumpy and scared, like I didn’t sleep at all. I can’t really say I’m reliving one specific thing, though.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your sleep is being interrupted by specific dreams or thoughts that upset you, or if you're having trouble falling asleep because your mind won't slow down?\n\nPatient: It’s more that my mind won’t slow down, I think. Like I get off the overnight shift and I should be exhausted, because everybody else is starting their day and I’m just getting home, and I’ll lie down but I’m just… awake. Wide awake.\n\nSometimes I start thinking about one thing, then another thing, and then I’m listening to noises outside, or thinking I forgot to do something, and then it’s already later than I meant it to be. And if I do finally drift off, it doesn’t feel like real sleep. I’ll wake up scared, like something happened, but I don’t always remember a dream or anything specific.\n\nThat’s kind of why I came in, I guess. My sleep is all broken up now. I wake up feeling like I never rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you still enjoy the things you used to, like going to church or helping with the kids at the center, or if those things don’t bring you the same happiness anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, church is still church, you know? I grew up going, my aunt used to make sure we were dressed right and on time, even if somebody’s shoes were missing. So it’s not like I don’t care about it.\n\nAnd the kids at the center… kids are funny, they’ll say anything. One little boy used to call me “Miss Snacks” because I always knew where the crackers were. I remember that.\n\nBut lately… I’m not sure if I’d say it doesn’t make me happy, exactly. I guess I haven’t really been paying attention to it like that. Sometimes I go through the motions and then afterward I’m thinking about whether I did everything right, or if people noticed I was quiet. So… I don’t know. It’s hard for me to tell.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I keep thinking maybe it’s the schedule, because nights mess everything up, you know, and then the sun’s coming up and people are mowing lawns or my neighbor’s dog starts barking, and then I’m just awake again.\n\nBut when I do drift off, I don’t feel rested. It just keeps going around and around.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having nightmares or vivid dreams that make you feel like you're reliving a stressful or upsetting experience?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call them nightmares exactly… sometimes I don’t even remember what I was dreaming. It’s more like I finally fall asleep after my overnight shift, after just lying there staring at the wall, and then I wake up scared like something happened.\n\nAnd it’s strange because I’ll be so tired, like my body should just shut off, but it doesn’t. I keep thinking maybe it’s the schedule, because nights mess everything up, you know, and then the sun’s coming up and people are mowing lawns or my neighbor’s dog starts barking, and then I’m just awake again.\n\nBut when I do drift off, I don’t feel rested. Wide awake.\n\nSometimes I start thinking about one thing, then another thing, and then I’m listening to noises outside, or thinking I forgot to do something, and then it’s already later than I meant it to be. I grew up going, my aunt used to make sure we were dressed right and on time, even if somebody’s shoes were missing. So it’s not like I don’t care about it.\n\nAnd the kids at the center… kids are funny, they’ll say anything. One little boy used to call me “Miss Snacks” because I always knew where the crackers were. I remember that.\n\nBut lately… I’m not sure if I’d say it doesn’t make me happy, exactly. Sometimes I go through the motions and then afterward I’m thinking about whether I did everything right, or if people noticed I was quiet. So… I don’t know. It’s hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: When you go to church or help with the kids at the center, do you find that you no longer feel the same sense of pleasure or enjoyment you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not really sure. I mean, sometimes I’m there and I know I’m supposed to feel something, especially at church when everybody’s singing and clapping, and I’ll be thinking about whether I parked in the right spot or if I left my badge in my work pants.\n\nWith the kids, I still know they’re sweet. Like if one of them draws a picture and says it’s me, that should be funny, right? And sometimes it is, I guess. I mean… Zumba is loud, you know? The music and everybody moving all at once, and I used to try to stand in the back so nobody could see if I missed the steps. My cousin, she laughs because she says I have no rhythm, but she’s the one who used to trip over the laundry basket at home, so I don’t know why she talks.

Can you tell me what brings you in today or what has been on your mind lately that made you decide to come in?\n\nPatient: I guess… mostly my sleep. It’s gotten all broken up, and I can’t seem to fix it. I work overnights, so you’d think when I get off I’d be exhausted and just knock out, but I’ll lie there wide awake, like my body forgot how to sleep. And then when I finally do drift off, I wake up scared, like something happened, and I don’t feel rested at all.\n\nIt’s been messing with me. I used to be able to sleep after a shift if the room was dark enough—my neighbor’s dog used to bark in the mornings and I’d still sleep through it sometimes—but now it’s like even quiet doesn’t help. That’s why I came in, I think. I just feel like I’m not really sleeping anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood, like feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I do feel down. Most days it’s like I wake up already heavy, like there’s this empty feeling sitting on me before I even get out of bed.\n\nI used to go to church, and I used to do Zumba sometimes—my cousin kept telling me I had no rhythm, but I’d still go—and I helped with the kids at the center too. I don’t know… it feels like that version of me is just gone now. Like I open my eyes and she’s not there anymore.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how often you feel this way throughout the day? Does it seem to come and go, or is it pretty much there all the time?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s pretty much there most days. Like from the minute I open my eyes, I already feel heavy and empty, like I’m carrying something before anything even happens.\n\nSometimes I’ll think about how I used to get up for church, or go to Zumba even when I didn’t feel like it—there was this one lady who always stood in the front like she was on TV—and I used to help with the kids at the center too. Now it’s like that person is just gone. So, yeah… it doesn’t really come and go much. It’s just kind of there.\n\nDoctor: Have you gone through any particularly stressful or traumatic experience recently that might be connected to your sleep problems or the way you feel when you wake up scared?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like one big thing that happened, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI mean, work is stressful, but it’s always kind of been that way. People needing things all night, call lights, the same hallway lights buzzing… there’s this one machine at work that makes this little clicking sound and it gets stuck in my head sometimes, but I don’t know if that counts.\n\nI just wake up scared and I can’t always tell why. It’s not like I can point to something and say, “That’s it.” I wish I could, because then maybe it would make more sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself thinking about a specific moment or event over and over again, or having sudden memories that feel very intense and upsetting, even if you can’t point to one big thing that happened?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really a specific moment, no. It’s not like a movie scene that keeps playing or anything.\n\nSometimes my mind gets stuck on little things, but not like… I don’t know, not one big memory. Like I’ll think about work stuff, or I’ll remember something small someone said, and then somehow I’m thinking about whether I left laundry in the washer, and then I’m thinking about how my aunt used to get mad if clothes smelled damp… but that’s not what you mean, right?\n\nI just wake up upset sometimes, but I can’t point to one thing that’s causing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find your mind going over the same thoughts or worries, even when you're trying to rest or do something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, my mind does that a lot. Especially when I’m trying to lay down or even just sit quiet, it starts going over every little thing I think I messed up.\n\nLike with patients, I’ll think, did I turn them right, did I miss something, did I answer too slow… and then it’ll jump to my family, like things I should’ve said different or done better. My aunt used to say if you’re caring for somebody, you don’t get to be careless, and I keep hearing stuff like that in my head.\n\nAnd then it feels like… I don’t know. Like I’m not a good aide. Like I’m worthless at it. And sometimes it feels like God is mad at me because I didn’t protect people better, even when I can’t even name exactly what I was supposed to protect them from. It just keeps going around and around.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having nightmares or vivid dreams that make you feel like you're reliving a stressful or upsetting experience?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call them nightmares exactly… sometimes I don’t even remember what I was dreaming. It’s more like I finally fall asleep after my overnight shift, after just lying there staring at the wall, and then I wake up scared like something happened.\n\nAnd it’s strange because I’ll be so tired, like my body should just shut off, but it doesn’t. I keep thinking maybe it’s the schedule, because nights mess everything up, you know, and then the sun’s coming up and people are mowing lawns or my neighbor’s dog starts barking, and then I’m just awake again.\n\nBut when I do drift off, I don’t feel rested. I wake up jumpy and scared, like I didn’t sleep at all. I can’t really say I’m reliving one specific thing, though.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your sleep is being interrupted by specific dreams or thoughts that upset you, or if you're having trouble falling asleep because your mind won't slow down?\n\nPatient: It’s more that my mind won’t slow down, I think. Like I get off the overnight shift and I should be exhausted, because everybody else is starting their day and I’m just getting home, and I’ll lie down but I’m just… awake. Wide awake.\n\nSometimes I start thinking about one thing, then another thing, and then I’m listening to noises outside, or thinking I forgot to do something, and then it’s already later than I meant it to be. And if I do finally drift off, it doesn’t feel like real sleep. I’ll wake up scared, like something happened, but I don’t always remember a dream or anything specific.\n\nThat’s kind of why I came in, I guess. My sleep is all broken up now. I wake up feeling like I never rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you still enjoy the things you used to, like going to church or helping with the kids at the center, or if those things don’t bring you the same happiness anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, church is still church, you know? I grew up going, my aunt used to make sure we were dressed right and on time, even if somebody’s shoes were missing. So it’s not like I don’t care about it.\n\nAnd the kids at the center… kids are funny, they’ll say anything. One little boy used to call me “Miss Snacks” because I always knew where the crackers were. I remember that.\n\nBut lately… I’m not sure if I’d say it doesn’t make me happy, exactly. I guess I haven’t really been paying attention to it like that. Sometimes I go through the motions and then afterward I’m thinking about whether I did everything right, or if people noticed I was quiet. So… I don’t know. It’s hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: When you go to church or help with the kids at the center, do you find that you no longer feel the same sense of pleasure or enjoyment you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not really sure. I mean, sometimes I’m there and I know I’m supposed to feel something, especially at church when everybody’s singing and clapping, and I’ll be thinking about whether I parked in the right spot or if I left my badge in my work pants.\n\nWith the kids, I still know they’re sweet. Like if one of them draws a picture and says it’s me, that should be funny, right? And sometimes it is, I guess. But I don’t always stop and think, “am I enjoying this?” I’m mostly just trying to get through what I’m supposed to do.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s gone or if I’m just not paying attention right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it’s harder to focus or think clearly lately, like you used to be able to do?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe? I mean, I never was the best with, like, paperwork stuff. In school I used to get lost when teachers talked too fast, and my cousin would say, “You just gotta write it down,” but then I’d lose the paper, so…\n\nLately I guess I’ll start doing something and then I’m thinking about something else, but I don’t know if that’s focus or just being tired. Like I can still do things if I really have to, I think. It just feels like my head is kind of crowded sometimes. I’m not sure if that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: When you go to Zumba or spend time with the kids at the center, do you find that you don’t feel the same joy or interest in those activities anymore?\n\nPatient: I mean… Zumba is loud, you know? The music and everybody moving all at once, and I used to try to stand in the back so nobody could see if I missed the steps. My cousin, she laughs because she says I have no rhythm, but she’s the one who used to trip over the laundry basket at home, so I don’t know why she talks.\n\nWith the kids at the center… they still do little things that should make you smile. Like when they argue over whose turn it is to pass out cups, like it’s some big important job. I notice that.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’d say I don’t feel joy anymore. I don’t really think about it that way while I’m there. Sometimes I’m just doing what I’m supposed to do, keeping up, making sure nothing goes wrong. So… I’m not sure. I can’t really tell if it’s different or if I’m just distracted.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t really know how to measure that. Like, Zumba was never something I was… I mean, I went because my cousin wanted me to go, and she’d be like, “Come on, it’s good for you,” and then afterward she’d want to get those smoothies that cost too much.

With the kids, I still show up and I do what I’m supposed to do. Sometimes they say something funny, or they get all excited over stickers, and I notice it. But I don’t sit there thinking, “Am I satisfied?” I’m mostly just trying to keep things in order.

So I guess… I don’t know. Maybe it feels different, but I can’t really say for sure.

Especially when I’m trying to lay down or even just sit quiet, it starts going over every little thing I think I messed up.\n\nLike with patients, I’ll think, did I turn them right, did I miss something, did I answer too slow… and then it’ll jump to my family, like things I should’ve said different or done better. My aunt used to say if you’re caring for somebody, you don’t get to be careless, and I keep hearing stuff like that in my head.\n\nAnd then it feels like… I don’t know. Like I’m not a good aide. I wake up jumpy and scared, like I didn’t sleep at all. I can’t really say I’m reliving one specific thing, though.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your sleep is being interrupted by specific dreams or thoughts that upset you, or if you're having trouble falling asleep because your mind won't slow down?\n\nPatient: It’s more that my mind won’t slow down, I think. Like I get off the overnight shift and I should be exhausted, because everybody else is starting their day and I’m just getting home, and I’ll lie down but I’m just… awake. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not sure… I know you keep asking it kind of the same way, and I’m trying to answer right, I just don’t really keep track of “interesting” like that.

Zumba is… it’s Zumba. Sometimes the instructor does those fast turns and I’m more worried about looking stupid than whether I’m enjoying it. And with the kids, I mean, kids are kids — one minute they’re sweet and then somebody’s crying over a crayon, and then you’re wiping a table and trying to remember where the extra cups are. So I don’t know if it feels good or not, exactly.

I guess I still go through the motions. I haven’t really sat down and thought, “Do I like this less now?” It’s hard for me to tell.

I used to be able to sleep after a shift if the room was dark enough—my neighbor’s dog used to bark in the mornings and I’d still sleep through it sometimes—but now it’s like even quiet doesn’t help. I don’t know… it feels like that version of me is just gone now. Like I open my eyes and she’s not there anymore.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how often you feel this way throughout the day? Does it seem to come and go, or is it pretty much there all the time?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s pretty much there most days. Now it’s like that person is just gone. So, yeah… it doesn’t really come and go much. It’s just kind of there.\n\nDoctor: Have you gone through any particularly stressful or traumatic experience recently that might be connected to your sleep problems or the way you feel when you wake up scared?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like one big thing that happened, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI mean, work is stressful, but it’s always kind of been that way. People needing things all night, call lights, the same hallway lights buzzing… there’s this one machine at work that makes this little clicking sound and it gets stuck in my head sometimes, but I don’t know if that counts.\n\nI just wake up scared and I can’t always tell why. It’s not like I can point to something and say, “That’s it.” I wish I could, because then maybe it would make more sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself thinking about a specific moment or event over and over again, or having sudden memories that feel very intense and upsetting, even if you can’t point to one big thing that happened?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really a specific moment, no. It’s not like a movie scene that keeps playing or anything.\n\nSometimes my mind gets stuck on little things, but not like… I don’t know, not one big memory. Like I’ll think about work stuff, or I’ll remember something small someone said, and then somehow I’m thinking about whether I left laundry in the washer, and then I’m thinking about how my aunt used to get mad if clothes smelled damp… but that’s not what you mean, right?\n\nI just wake up upset sometimes, but I can’t point to one thing that’s causing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find your mind going over the same thoughts or worries, even when you're trying to rest or do something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, my mind does that a lot. Especially when I’m trying to lay down or even just sit quiet, it starts going over every little thing I think I messed up.\n\nLike with patients, I’ll think, did I turn them right, did I miss something, did I answer too slow… and then it’ll jump to my family, like things I should’ve said different or done better. My aunt used to say if you’re caring for somebody, you don’t get to be careless, and I keep hearing stuff like that in my head.\n\nAnd then it feels like… I don’t know. Like I’m not a good aide. Like I’m worthless at it. And sometimes it feels like God is mad at me because I didn’t protect people better, even when I can’t even name exactly what I was supposed to protect them from. It just keeps going around and around.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having nightmares or vivid dreams that make you feel like you're reliving a stressful or upsetting experience?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call them nightmares exactly… sometimes I don’t even remember what I was dreaming. It’s more like I finally fall asleep after my overnight shift, after just lying there staring at the wall, and then I wake up scared like something happened.\n\nAnd it’s strange because I’ll be so tired, like my body should just shut off, but it doesn’t. I keep thinking maybe it’s the schedule, because nights mess everything up, you know, and then the sun’s coming up and people are mowing lawns or my neighbor’s dog starts barking, and then I’m just awake again.\n\nBut when I do drift off, I don’t feel rested. I wake up jumpy and scared, like I didn’t sleep at all. I can’t really say I’m reliving one specific thing, though.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your sleep is being interrupted by specific dreams or thoughts that upset you, or if you're having trouble falling asleep because your mind won't slow down?\n\nPatient: It’s more that my mind won’t slow down, I think. Like I get off the overnight shift and I should be exhausted, because everybody else is starting their day and I’m just getting home, and I’ll lie down but I’m just… awake. Wide awake.\n\nSometimes I start thinking about one thing, then another thing, and then I’m listening to noises outside, or thinking I forgot to do something, and then it’s already later than I meant it to be. And if I do finally drift off, it doesn’t feel like real sleep. I’ll wake up scared, like something happened, but I don’t always remember a dream or anything specific.\n\nThat’s kind of why I came in, I guess. My sleep is all broken up now. I wake up feeling like I never rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you still enjoy the things you used to, like going to church or helping with the kids at the center, or if those things don’t bring you the same happiness anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, church is still church, you know? I grew up going, my aunt used to make sure we were dressed right and on time, even if somebody’s shoes were missing. So it’s not like I don’t care about it.\n\nAnd the kids at the center… kids are funny, they’ll say anything. One little boy used to call me “Miss Snacks” because I always knew where the crackers were. I remember that.\n\nBut lately… I’m not sure if I’d say it doesn’t make me happy, exactly. Sometimes I go through the motions and then afterward I’m thinking about whether I did everything right, or if people noticed I was quiet. So… I don’t know. It’s hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: When you go to church or help with the kids at the center, do you find that you no longer feel the same sense of pleasure or enjoyment you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not really sure. Like if one of them draws a picture and says it’s me, that should be funny, right? And sometimes it is, I guess. But I don’t always stop and think, “am I enjoying this?” I’m mostly just trying to get through what I’m supposed to do.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s gone or if I’m just not paying attention right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it’s harder to focus or think clearly lately, like you used to be able to do?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe? I mean, I never was the best with, like, paperwork stuff. Now it’s like that person is just gone. So, yeah… it doesn’t really come and go much. It’s just kind of there.\n\nDoctor: Have you gone through any particularly stressful or traumatic experience recently that might be connected to your sleep problems or the way you feel when you wake up scared?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like one big thing that happened, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI mean, work is stressful, but it’s always kind of been that way. People needing things all night, call lights, the same hallway lights buzzing… there’s this one machine at work that makes this little clicking sound and it gets stuck in my head sometimes, but I don’t know if that counts.\n\nI just wake up scared and I can’t always tell why. It’s not like I can point to something and say, “That’s it.” I wish I could, because then maybe it would make more sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself thinking about a specific moment or event over and over again, or having sudden memories that feel very intense and upsetting, even if you can’t point to one big thing that happened?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really a specific moment, no. It’s not like a movie scene that keeps playing or anything.\n\nSometimes my mind gets stuck on little things, but not like… I don’t know, not one big memory. Like I’ll think about work stuff, or I’ll remember something small someone said, and then somehow I’m thinking about whether I left laundry in the washer, and then I’m thinking about how my aunt used to get mad if clothes smelled damp… but that’s not what you mean, right?\n\nI just wake up upset sometimes, but I can’t point to one thing that’s causing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find your mind going over the same thoughts or worries, even when you're trying to rest or do something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, my mind does that a lot. Especially when I’m trying to lay down or even just sit quiet, it starts going over every little thing I think I messed up.\n\nLike with patients, I’ll think, did I turn them right, did I miss something, did I answer too slow… and then it’ll jump to my family, like things I should’ve said different or done better. My aunt used to say if you’re caring for somebody, you don’t get to be careless, and I keep hearing stuff like that in my head.\n\nAnd then it feels like… I don’t know. Like I’m not a good aide. Like I’m worthless at it. And sometimes it feels like God is mad at me because I didn’t protect people better, even when I can’t even name exactly what I was supposed to protect them from. It just keeps going around and around.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having nightmares or vivid dreams that make you feel like you're reliving a stressful or upsetting experience?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call them nightmares exactly… sometimes I don’t even remember what I was dreaming. It’s more like I finally fall asleep after my overnight shift, after just lying there staring at the wall, and then I wake up scared like something happened.\n\nAnd it’s strange because I’ll be so tired, like my body should just shut off, but it doesn’t. I keep thinking maybe it’s the schedule, because nights mess everything up, you know, and then the sun’s coming up and people are mowing lawns or my neighbor’s dog starts barking, and then I’m just awake again.\n\nBut when I do drift off, I don’t feel rested. I wake up jumpy and scared, like I didn’t sleep at all. I can’t really say I’m reliving one specific thing, though.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your sleep is being interrupted by specific dreams or thoughts that upset you, or if you're having trouble falling asleep because your mind won't slow down?\n\nPatient: It’s more that my mind won’t slow down, I think. Like I get off the overnight shift and I should be exhausted, because everybody else is starting their day and I’m just getting home, and I’ll lie down but I’m just… awake. Wide awake.\n\nSometimes I start thinking about one thing, then another thing, and then I’m listening to noises outside, or thinking I forgot to do something, and then it’s already later than I meant it to be. And if I do finally drift off, it doesn’t feel like real sleep. I’ll wake up scared, like something happened, but I don’t always remember a dream or anything specific.\n\nThat’s kind of why I came in, I guess. My sleep is all broken up now. I wake up feeling like I never rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you still enjoy the things you used to, like going to church or helping with the kids at the center, or if those things don’t bring you the same happiness anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, church is still church, you know? I grew up going, my aunt used to make sure we were dressed right and on time, even if somebody’s shoes were missing. So it’s not like I don’t care about it.\n\nAnd the kids at the center… kids are funny, they’ll say anything. One little boy used to call me “Miss Snacks” because I always knew where the crackers were. I remember that.\n\nBut lately… I’m not sure if I’d say it doesn’t make me happy, exactly. I guess I haven’t really been paying attention to it like that. Sometimes I go through the motions and then afterward I’m thinking about whether I did everything right, or if people noticed I was quiet. So… I don’t know. It’s hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: When you go to church or help with the kids at the center, do you find that you no longer feel the same sense of pleasure or enjoyment you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not really sure. I mean, sometimes I’m there and I know I’m supposed to feel something, especially at church when everybody’s singing and clapping, and I’ll be thinking about whether I parked in the right spot or if I left my badge in my work pants.\n\nWith the kids, I still know they’re sweet. Like if one of them draws a picture and says it’s me, that should be funny, right? And sometimes it is, I guess. But I don’t always stop and think, “am I enjoying this?” I’m mostly just trying to get through what I’m supposed to do.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s gone or if I’m just not paying attention right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it’s harder to focus or think clearly lately, like you used to be able to do?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe? I mean, I never was the best with, like, paperwork stuff. In school I used to get lost when teachers talked too fast, and my cousin would say, “You just gotta write it down,” but then I’d lose the paper, so…\n\nLately I guess I’ll start doing something and then I’m thinking about something else, but I don’t know if that’s focus or just being tired. Like I can still do things if I really have to, I think. It just feels like my head is kind of crowded sometimes. I’m not sure if that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: When you go to Zumba or spend time with the kids at the center, do you find that you don’t feel the same joy or interest in those activities anymore?\n\nPatient: I mean… Zumba is loud, you know? The music and everybody moving all at once, and I used to try to stand in the back so nobody could see if I missed the steps. My cousin, she laughs because she says I have no rhythm, but she’s the one who used to trip over the laundry basket at home, so I don’t know why she talks.\n\nWith the kids at the center… they still do little things that should make you smile. Like when they argue over whose turn it is to pass out cups, like it’s some big important job. I notice that.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’d say I don’t feel joy anymore. I don’t really think about it that way while I’m there. Sometimes I’m just doing what I’m supposed to do, keeping up, making sure nothing goes wrong. So… I’m not sure. I can’t really tell if it’s different or if I’m just distracted.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that activities you used to look forward to, like Zumba or spending time with the kids, don’t bring you the same sense of pleasure or satisfaction anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to measure that. Like, Zumba was never something I was… I mean, I went because my cousin wanted me to go, and she’d be like, “Come on, it’s good for you,” and then afterward she’d want to get those smoothies that cost too much.\n\nWith the kids, I still show up and I do what I’m supposed to do. Sometimes they say something funny, or they get all excited over stickers, and I notice it. But I don’t sit there thinking, “Am I satisfied?” I’m mostly just trying to keep things in order.\n\nSo I guess… I don’t know. Maybe it feels different, but I can’t really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that things you used to really enjoy, like Zumba or being with the kids, don’t feel as good or as interesting to you anymore?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I know you keep asking it kind of the same way, and I’m trying to answer right, I just don’t really keep track of “interesting” like that.\n\nZumba is… it’s Zumba. Sometimes the instructor does those fast turns and I’m more worried about looking stupid than whether I’m enjoying it. And with the kids, I mean, kids are kids — one minute they’re sweet and then somebody’s crying over a crayon, and then you’re wiping a table and trying to remember where the extra cups are. So I don’t know if it feels good or not, exactly.\n\nI guess I still go through the motions. I haven’t really sat down and thought, “Do I like this less now?” It’s hard for me to tell.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t know… I feel like I already kind of said it, but I’m not trying to be difficult. It’s just hard to tell.

Like with Zumba, half the time I’m watching the lady in front of me because she knows all the steps, and then I forget which way my feet are supposed to go. Like I’ll think about work stuff, or I’ll remember something small someone said, and then somehow I’m thinking about whether I left laundry in the washer, and then I’m thinking about how my aunt used to get mad if clothes smelled damp… but that’s not what you mean, right?\n\nI just wake up upset sometimes, but I can’t point to one thing that’s causing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find your mind going over the same thoughts or worries, even when you're trying to rest or do something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, my mind does that a lot. Especially when I’m trying to lay down or even just sit quiet, it starts going over every little thing I think I messed up.\n\nLike with patients, I’ll think, did I turn them right, did I miss something, did I answer too slow… and then it’ll jump to my family, like things I should’ve said different or done better. My aunt used to say if you’re caring for somebody, you don’t get to be careless, and I keep hearing stuff like that in my head.\n\nAnd then it feels like… I don’t know. Like I’m not a good aide. Like I’m worthless at it. And sometimes it feels like God is mad at me because I didn’t protect people better, even when I can’t even name exactly what I was supposed to protect them from. It just keeps going around and around.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having nightmares or vivid dreams that make you feel like you're reliving a stressful or upsetting experience?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call them nightmares exactly… sometimes I don’t even remember what I was dreaming. It’s more like I finally fall asleep after my overnight shift, after just lying there staring at the wall, and then I wake up scared like something happened.\n\nAnd it’s strange because I’ll be so tired, like my body should just shut off, but it doesn’t. I keep thinking maybe it’s the schedule, because nights mess everything up, you know, and then the sun’s coming up and people are mowing lawns or my neighbor’s dog starts barking, and then I’m just awake again.\n\nBut when I do drift off, I don’t feel rested. I wake up jumpy and scared, like I didn’t sleep at all. I can’t really say I’m reliving one specific thing, though.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your sleep is being interrupted by specific dreams or thoughts that upset you, or if you're having trouble falling asleep because your mind won't slow down?\n\nPatient: It’s more that my mind won’t slow down, I think. Like I get off the overnight shift and I should be exhausted, because everybody else is starting their day and I’m just getting home, and I’ll lie down but I’m just… awake. Wide awake.\n\nSometimes I start thinking about one thing, then another thing, and then I’m listening to noises outside, or thinking I forgot to do something, and then it’s already later than I meant it to be. And if I do finally drift off, it doesn’t feel like real sleep. I’ll wake up scared, like something happened, but I don’t always remember a dream or anything specific.\n\nThat’s kind of why I came in, I guess. My sleep is all broken up now. I wake up feeling like I never rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you still enjoy the things you used to, like going to church or helping with the kids at the center, or if those things don’t bring you the same happiness anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, church is still church, you know? I grew up going, my aunt used to make sure we were dressed right and on time, even if somebody’s shoes were missing. So it’s not like I don’t care about it.\n\nAnd the kids at the center… kids are funny, they’ll say anything. One little boy used to call me “Miss Snacks” because I always knew where the crackers were. I remember that.\n\nBut lately… I’m not sure if I’d say it doesn’t make me happy, exactly. I guess I haven’t really been paying attention to it like that. Sometimes I go through the motions and then afterward I’m thinking about whether I did everything right, or if people noticed I was quiet. So… I don’t know. It’s hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: When you go to church or help with the kids at the center, do you find that you no longer feel the same sense of pleasure or enjoyment you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not really sure. I mean, sometimes I’m there and I know I’m supposed to feel something, especially at church when everybody’s singing and clapping, and I’ll be thinking about whether I parked in the right spot or if I left my badge in my work pants.\n\nWith the kids, I still know they’re sweet. Like if one of them draws a picture and says it’s me, that should be funny, right? And sometimes it is, I guess. But I don’t always stop and think, “am I enjoying this?” I’m mostly just trying to get through what I’m supposed to do.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s gone or if I’m just not paying attention right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it’s harder to focus or think clearly lately, like you used to be able to do?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe? I mean, I never was the best with, like, paperwork stuff. In school I used to get lost when teachers talked too fast, and my cousin would say, “You just gotta write it down,” but then I’d lose the paper, so…\n\nLately I guess I’ll start doing something and then I’m thinking about something else, but I don’t know if that’s focus or just being tired. Like I can still do things if I really have to, I think. It just feels like my head is kind of crowded sometimes. I’m not sure if that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: When you go to Zumba or spend time with the kids at the center, do you find that you don’t feel the same joy or interest in those activities anymore?\n\nPatient: I mean… Zumba is loud, you know? The music and everybody moving all at once, and I used to try to stand in the back so nobody could see if I missed the steps. My cousin, she laughs because she says I have no rhythm, but she’s the one who used to trip over the laundry basket at home, so I don’t know why she talks.\n\nWith the kids at the center… they still do little things that should make you smile. Like when they argue over whose turn it is to pass out cups, like it’s some big important job. I notice that.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’d say I don’t feel joy anymore. I don’t really think about it that way while I’m there. Sometimes I’m just doing what I’m supposed to do, keeping up, making sure nothing goes wrong. So… I’m not sure. I can’t really tell if it’s different or if I’m just distracted.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that activities you used to look forward to, like Zumba or spending time with the kids, don’t bring you the same sense of pleasure or satisfaction anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to measure that. Like, Zumba was never something I was… I mean, I went because my cousin wanted me to go, and she’d be like, “Come on, it’s good for you,” and then afterward she’d want to get those smoothies that cost too much.\n\nWith the kids, I still show up and I do what I’m supposed to do. Sometimes they say something funny, or they get all excited over stickers, and I notice it. But I don’t sit there thinking, “Am I satisfied?” I’m mostly just trying to keep things in order.\n\nSo I guess… I don’t know. Maybe it feels different, but I can’t really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that things you used to really enjoy, like Zumba or being with the kids, don’t feel as good or as interesting to you anymore?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I know you keep asking it kind of the same way, and I’m trying to answer right, I just don’t really keep track of “interesting” like that.\n\nZumba is… it’s Zumba. Sometimes the instructor does those fast turns and I’m more worried about looking stupid than whether I’m enjoying it. And with the kids, I mean, kids are kids — one minute they’re sweet and then somebody’s crying over a crayon, and then you’re wiping a table and trying to remember where the extra cups are. So I don’t know if it feels good or not, exactly.\n\nI guess I still go through the motions. I haven’t really sat down and thought, “Do I like this less now?” It’s hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that things you used to enjoy, like Zumba or being with the kids, don’t feel as good or interesting to you anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I feel like I already kind of said it, but I’m not trying to be difficult. It’s just hard to tell.\n\nLike with Zumba, half the time I’m watching the lady in front of me because she knows all the steps, and then I forget which way my feet are supposed to go. And the room gets hot, and somebody always has one of those water bottles that makes a loud cracking sound when they squeeze it—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I haven’t noticed it in a clear way, like, “Oh, this doesn’t feel good anymore.” With the kids, I still notice when they’re being funny or when they’re proud of something. I just don’t really know if it feels the same as before. I don’t have a good answer for that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

And the room gets hot, and somebody always has one of those water bottles that makes a loud cracking sound when they squeeze it—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I haven’t noticed it in a clear way, like, “Oh, this doesn’t feel good anymore.” With the kids, I still notice when they’re being funny or when they’re proud of something. I just don’t really know if it feels the same as before. I don’t have a good answer for that.\n\n[Last Question]\nCan you tell me if you’ve lost pleasure or interest in things you used to enjoy, like Zumba or spending time with the kids?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… I don’t know how to say it different. I haven’t really sat there and thought, “I lost pleasure,” you know? That sounds so definite.

Zumba was always kind of my cousin’s thing more than mine, and sometimes I’d go mostly so she wouldn’t keep texting me about it. And with the kids, there’s always something happening — snacks, wiping noses, somebody needing help with shoes — so it’s not like I’m checking if I’m enjoying it.

I guess I can’t really tell. Maybe it’s not the same, maybe it is. I just don’t have a clear answer like that.

    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.9, F43.10\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome. Can you tell me what brings you in today or what has been on your mind lately that made you decide to come in?\n\nPatient: I guess… mostly my sleep. It’s gotten all broken up, and I can’t seem to fix it. I work overnights, so you’d think when I get off I’d be exhausted and just knock out, but I’ll lie there wide awake, like my body forgot how to sleep. And then when I finally do drift off, I wake up scared, like something happened, and I don’t feel rested at all.\n\nIt’s been messing with me. I used to be able to sleep after a shift if the room was dark enough—my neighbor’s dog used to bark in the mornings and I’d still sleep through it sometimes—but now it’s like even quiet doesn’t help. That’s why I came in, I think. I just feel like I’m not really sleeping anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood, like feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I do feel down. Most days it’s like I wake up already heavy, like there’s this empty feeling sitting on me before I even get out of bed.\n\nI used to go to church, and I used to do Zumba sometimes—my cousin kept telling me I had no rhythm, but I’d still go—and I helped with the kids at the center too. I don’t know… it feels like that version of me is just gone now. Like I open my eyes and she’s not there anymore.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how often you feel this way throughout the day? Does it seem to come and go, or is it pretty much there all the time?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s pretty much there most days. Like from the minute I open my eyes, I already feel heavy and empty, like I’m carrying something before anything even happens.\n\nSometimes I’ll think about how I used to get up for church, or go to Zumba even when I didn’t feel like it—there was this one lady who always stood in the front like she was on TV—and I used to help with the kids at the center too. Now it’s like that person is just gone. So, yeah… it doesn’t really come and go much. It’s just kind of there.\n\nDoctor: Have you gone through any particularly stressful or traumatic experience recently that might be connected to your sleep problems or the way you feel when you wake up scared?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like one big thing that happened, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI mean, work is stressful, but it’s always kind of been that way. People needing things all night, call lights, the same hallway lights buzzing… there’s this one machine at work that makes this little clicking sound and it gets stuck in my head sometimes, but I don’t know if that counts.\n\nI just wake up scared and I can’t always tell why. It’s not like I can point to something and say, “That’s it.” I wish I could, because then maybe it would make more sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself thinking about a specific moment or event over and over again, or having sudden memories that feel very intense and upsetting, even if you can’t point to one big thing that happened?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really a specific moment, no. It’s not like a movie scene that keeps playing or anything.\n\nSometimes my mind gets stuck on little things, but not like… I don’t know, not one big memory. Like I’ll think about work stuff, or I’ll remember something small someone said, and then somehow I’m thinking about whether I left laundry in the washer, and then I’m thinking about how my aunt used to get mad if clothes smelled damp… but that’s not what you mean, right?\n\nI just wake up upset sometimes, but I can’t point to one thing that’s causing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find your mind going over the same thoughts or worries, even when you're trying to rest or do something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, my mind does that a lot. Especially when I’m trying to lay down or even just sit quiet, it starts going over every little thing I think I messed up.\n\nLike with patients, I’ll think, did I turn them right, did I miss something, did I answer too slow… and then it’ll jump to my family, like things I should’ve said different or done better. My aunt used to say if you’re caring for somebody, you don’t get to be careless, and I keep hearing stuff like that in my head.\n\nAnd then it feels like… I don’t know. Like I’m not a good aide. Like I’m worthless at it. And sometimes it feels like God is mad at me because I didn’t protect people better, even when I can’t even name exactly what I was supposed to protect them from. It just keeps going around and around.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having nightmares or vivid dreams that make you feel like you're reliving a stressful or upsetting experience?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call them nightmares exactly… sometimes I don’t even remember what I was dreaming. It’s more like I finally fall asleep after my overnight shift, after just lying there staring at the wall, and then I wake up scared like something happened.\n\nAnd it’s strange because I’ll be so tired, like my body should just shut off, but it doesn’t. I keep thinking maybe it’s the schedule, because nights mess everything up, you know, and then the sun’s coming up and people are mowing lawns or my neighbor’s dog starts barking, and then I’m just awake again.\n\nBut when I do drift off, I don’t feel rested. I wake up jumpy and scared, like I didn’t sleep at all. I can’t really say I’m reliving one specific thing, though.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your sleep is being interrupted by specific dreams or thoughts that upset you, or if you're having trouble falling asleep because your mind won't slow down?\n\nPatient: It’s more that my mind won’t slow down, I think. Like I get off the overnight shift and I should be exhausted, because everybody else is starting their day and I’m just getting home, and I’ll lie down but I’m just… awake. Wide awake.\n\nSometimes I start thinking about one thing, then another thing, and then I’m listening to noises outside, or thinking I forgot to do something, and then it’s already later than I meant it to be. And if I do finally drift off, it doesn’t feel like real sleep. I’ll wake up scared, like something happened, but I don’t always remember a dream or anything specific.\n\nThat’s kind of why I came in, I guess. My sleep is all broken up now. I wake up feeling like I never rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you still enjoy the things you used to, like going to church or helping with the kids at the center, or if those things don’t bring you the same happiness anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, church is still church, you know? I grew up going, my aunt used to make sure we were dressed right and on time, even if somebody’s shoes were missing. So it’s not like I don’t care about it.\n\nAnd the kids at the center… kids are funny, they’ll say anything. One little boy used to call me “Miss Snacks” because I always knew where the crackers were. I remember that.\n\nBut lately… I’m not sure if I’d say it doesn’t make me happy, exactly. I guess I haven’t really been paying attention to it like that. Sometimes I go through the motions and then afterward I’m thinking about whether I did everything right, or if people noticed I was quiet. So… I don’t know. It’s hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: When you go to church or help with the kids at the center, do you find that you no longer feel the same sense of pleasure or enjoyment you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not really sure. I mean, sometimes I’m there and I know I’m supposed to feel something, especially at church when everybody’s singing and clapping, and I’ll be thinking about whether I parked in the right spot or if I left my badge in my work pants.\n\nWith the kids, I still know they’re sweet. Like if one of them draws a picture and says it’s me, that should be funny, right? And sometimes it is, I guess. But I don’t always stop and think, “am I enjoying this?” I’m mostly just trying to get through what I’m supposed to do.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s gone or if I’m just not paying attention right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it’s harder to focus or think clearly lately, like you used to be able to do?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe? I mean, I never was the best with, like, paperwork stuff. In school I used to get lost when teachers talked too fast, and my cousin would say, “You just gotta write it down,” but then I’d lose the paper, so…\n\nLately I guess I’ll start doing something and then I’m thinking about something else, but I don’t know if that’s focus or just being tired. Like I can still do things if I really have to, I think. It just feels like my head is kind of crowded sometimes. I’m not sure if that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: When you go to Zumba or spend time with the kids at the center, do you find that you don’t feel the same joy or interest in those activities anymore?\n\nPatient: I mean… Zumba is loud, you know? The music and everybody moving all at once, and I used to try to stand in the back so nobody could see if I missed the steps. My cousin, she laughs because she says I have no rhythm, but she’s the one who used to trip over the laundry basket at home, so I don’t know why she talks.\n\nWith the kids at the center… they still do little things that should make you smile. Like when they argue over whose turn it is to pass out cups, like it’s some big important job. I notice that.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’d say I don’t feel joy anymore. I don’t really think about it that way while I’m there. Sometimes I’m just doing what I’m supposed to do, keeping up, making sure nothing goes wrong. So… I’m not sure. I can’t really tell if it’s different or if I’m just distracted.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that activities you used to look forward to, like Zumba or spending time with the kids, don’t bring you the same sense of pleasure or satisfaction anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to measure that. Like, Zumba was never something I was… I mean, I went because my cousin wanted me to go, and she’d be like, “Come on, it’s good for you,” and then afterward she’d want to get those smoothies that cost too much.\n\nWith the kids, I still show up and I do what I’m supposed to do. Sometimes they say something funny, or they get all excited over stickers, and I notice it. But I don’t sit there thinking, “Am I satisfied?” I’m mostly just trying to keep things in order.\n\nSo I guess… I don’t know. Maybe it feels different, but I can’t really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that things you used to really enjoy, like Zumba or being with the kids, don’t feel as good or as interesting to you anymore?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I know you keep asking it kind of the same way, and I’m trying to answer right, I just don’t really keep track of “interesting” like that.\n\nZumba is… it’s Zumba. Sometimes the instructor does those fast turns and I’m more worried about looking stupid than whether I’m enjoying it. I wake up feeling like I never rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you still enjoy the things you used to, like going to church or helping with the kids at the center, or if those things don’t bring you the same happiness anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, church is still church, you know? I grew up going, my aunt used to make sure we were dressed right and on time, even if somebody’s shoes were missing. So it’s not like I don’t care about it.\n\nAnd the kids at the center… kids are funny, they’ll say anything. One little boy used to call me “Miss Snacks” because I always knew where the crackers were. I remember that.\n\nBut lately… I’m not sure if I’d say it doesn’t make me happy, exactly. I guess I haven’t really been paying attention to it like that. Sometimes I go through the motions and then afterward I’m thinking about whether I did everything right, or if people noticed I was quiet. So… I don’t know. It’s hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: When you go to church or help with the kids at the center, do you find that you no longer feel the same sense of pleasure or enjoyment you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not really sure. I mean, sometimes I’m there and I know I’m supposed to feel something, especially at church when everybody’s singing and clapping, and I’ll be thinking about whether I parked in the right spot or if I left my badge in my work pants.\n\nWith the kids, I still know they’re sweet. Like if one of them draws a picture and says it’s me, that should be funny, right? And sometimes it is, I guess. But I don’t always stop and think, “am I enjoying this?” I’m mostly just trying to get through what I’m supposed to do.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s gone or if I’m just not paying attention right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it’s harder to focus or think clearly lately, like you used to be able to do?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe? I mean, I never was the best with, like, paperwork stuff. In school I used to get lost when teachers talked too fast, and my cousin would say, “You just gotta write it down,” but then I’d lose the paper, so…\n\nLately I guess I’ll start doing something and then I’m thinking about something else, but I don’t know if that’s focus or just being tired. Like I can still do things if I really have to, I think. It just feels like my head is kind of crowded sometimes. I’m not sure if that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: When you go to Zumba or spend time with the kids at the center, do you find that you don’t feel the same joy or interest in those activities anymore?\n\nPatient: I mean… Zumba is loud, you know? The music and everybody moving all at once, and I used to try to stand in the back so nobody could see if I missed the steps. My cousin, she laughs because she says I have no rhythm, but she’s the one who used to trip over the laundry basket at home, so I don’t know why she talks.\n\nWith the kids at the center… they still do little things that should make you smile. Like when they argue over whose turn it is to pass out cups, like it’s some big important job. I notice that.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’d say I don’t feel joy anymore. I don’t really think about it that way while I’m there. Sometimes I’m just doing what I’m supposed to do, keeping up, making sure nothing goes wrong. So… I’m not sure. I can’t really tell if it’s different or if I’m just distracted.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that activities you used to look forward to, like Zumba or spending time with the kids, don’t bring you the same sense of pleasure or satisfaction anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to measure that. Like, Zumba was never something I was… I mean, I went because my cousin wanted me to go, and she’d be like, “Come on, it’s good for you,” and then afterward she’d want to get those smoothies that cost too much.\n\nWith the kids, I still show up and I do what I’m supposed to do. Sometimes they say something funny, or they get all excited over stickers, and I notice it. But I don’t sit there thinking, “Am I satisfied?” I’m mostly just trying to keep things in order.\n\nSo I guess… I don’t know. Maybe it feels different, but I can’t really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that things you used to really enjoy, like Zumba or being with the kids, don’t feel as good or as interesting to you anymore?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I know you keep asking it kind of the same way, and I’m trying to answer right, I just don’t really keep track of “interesting” like that.\n\nZumba is… it’s Zumba. Sometimes the instructor does those fast turns and I’m more worried about looking stupid than whether I’m enjoying it. And with the kids, I mean, kids are kids — one minute they’re sweet and then somebody’s crying over a crayon, and then you’re wiping a table and trying to remember where the extra cups are. So I don’t know if it feels good or not, exactly.\n\nI guess I still go through the motions. I haven’t really sat down and thought, “Do I like this less now?” It’s hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that things you used to enjoy, like Zumba or being with the kids, don’t feel as good or interesting to you anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I feel like I already kind of said it, but I’m not trying to be difficult. I’m… I’m not sure. “Don’t care as much” is hard because sometimes I go and do the thing anyway, so I don’t know what that means.

Sometimes I go through the motions and then afterward I’m thinking about whether I did everything right, or if people noticed I was quiet. So… I don’t know. It’s hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: When you go to church or help with the kids at the center, do you find that you no longer feel the same sense of pleasure or enjoyment you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not really sure. I mean, sometimes I’m there and I know I’m supposed to feel something, especially at church when everybody’s singing and clapping, and I’ll be thinking about whether I parked in the right spot or if I left my badge in my work pants.\n\nWith the kids, I still know they’re sweet. Like if one of them draws a picture and says it’s me, that should be funny, right? And sometimes it is, I guess. But I don’t always stop and think, “am I enjoying this?” I’m mostly just trying to get through what I’m supposed to do.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s gone or if I’m just not paying attention right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it’s harder to focus or think clearly lately, like you used to be able to do?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe? I mean, I never was the best with, like, paperwork stuff. In school I used to get lost when teachers talked too fast, and my cousin would say, “You just gotta write it down,” but then I’d lose the paper, so…\n\nLately I guess I’ll start doing something and then I’m thinking about something else, but I don’t know if that’s focus or just being tired. Like I can still do things if I really have to, I think. It just feels like my head is kind of crowded sometimes. I’m not sure if that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: When you go to Zumba or spend time with the kids at the center, do you find that you don’t feel the same joy or interest in those activities anymore?\n\nPatient: I mean… Zumba is loud, you know? The music and everybody moving all at once, and I used to try to stand in the back so nobody could see if I missed the steps. My cousin, she laughs because she says I have no rhythm, but she’s the one who used to trip over the laundry basket at home, so I don’t know why she talks.\n\nWith the kids at the center… they still do little things that should make you smile. Like when they argue over whose turn it is to pass out cups, like it’s some big important job. I notice that.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’d say I don’t feel joy anymore. I don’t really think about it that way while I’m there. Sometimes I’m just doing what I’m supposed to do, keeping up, making sure nothing goes wrong. So… I’m not sure. I can’t really tell if it’s different or if I’m just distracted.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that activities you used to look forward to, like Zumba or spending time with the kids, don’t bring you the same sense of pleasure or satisfaction anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to measure that. Like, Zumba was never something I was… I mean, I went because my cousin wanted me to go, and she’d be like, “Come on, it’s good for you,” and then afterward she’d want to get those smoothies that cost too much.\n\nWith the kids, I still show up and I do what I’m supposed to do. Sometimes they say something funny, or they get all excited over stickers, and I notice it. But I don’t sit there thinking, “Am I satisfied?” I’m mostly just trying to keep things in order.\n\nSo I guess… I don’t know. Maybe it feels different, but I can’t really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that things you used to really enjoy, like Zumba or being with the kids, don’t feel as good or as interesting to you anymore?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I know you keep asking it kind of the same way, and I’m trying to answer right, I just don’t really keep track of “interesting” like that.\n\nZumba is… it’s Zumba. Sometimes the instructor does those fast turns and I’m more worried about looking stupid than whether I’m enjoying it. And with the kids, I mean, kids are kids — one minute they’re sweet and then somebody’s crying over a crayon, and then you’re wiping a table and trying to remember where the extra cups are. So I don’t know if it feels good or not, exactly.\n\nI guess I still go through the motions. I haven’t really sat down and thought, “Do I like this less now?” It’s hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that things you used to enjoy, like Zumba or being with the kids, don’t feel as good or interesting to you anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I feel like I already kind of said it, but I’m not trying to be difficult. It’s just hard to tell.\n\nLike with Zumba, half the time I’m watching the lady in front of me because she knows all the steps, and then I forget which way my feet are supposed to go. And the room gets hot, and somebody always has one of those water bottles that makes a loud cracking sound when they squeeze it—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I haven’t noticed it in a clear way, like, “Oh, this doesn’t feel good anymore.” With the kids, I still notice when they’re being funny or when they’re proud of something. I just don’t really know if it feels the same as before. I don’t have a good answer for that.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve lost pleasure or interest in things you used to enjoy, like Zumba or spending time with the kids?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know how to say it different. I haven’t really sat there and thought, “I lost pleasure,” you know? That sounds so definite.\n\nZumba was always kind of my cousin’s thing more than mine, and sometimes I’d go mostly so she wouldn’t keep texting me about it. And with the kids, there’s always something happening — snacks, wiping noses, somebody needing help with shoes — so it’s not like I’m checking if I’m enjoying it.\n\nI guess I can’t really tell. Maybe it’s not the same, maybe it is. I just don’t have a clear answer like that.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that you don’t care as much about doing things you used to like, such as Zumba or spending time with the kids?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure. “Don’t care as much” is hard because sometimes I go and do the thing anyway, so I don’t know what that means.\n\nLike Zumba, I’ll be thinking about my shoes squeaking on the floor or whether I parked too close to that one truck outside, and then the song changes and I’m behind everybody. And with the kids, it’s not like I’m deciding if I care — I’m just doing what needs doing, you know? Getting them settled, making sure nobody’s sticking glue on the wall.\n\nSo I don’t really have a clear yes or no. I haven’t noticed it like that exactly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don’t really know… like, before Zumba I’m usually thinking, “Do I have clean leggings?” or “Where did I put my keys?” and then my cousin texts me something with a bunch of exclamation points, and I’m already annoyed about getting out the door.

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.9, F43.10\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome. Can you tell me what brings you in today or what has been on your mind lately that made you decide to come in?\n\nPatient: I guess… mostly my sleep. It’s gotten all broken up, and I can’t seem to fix it. I work overnights, so you’d think when I get off I’d be exhausted and just knock out, but I’ll lie there wide awake, like my body forgot how to sleep. And then when I finally do drift off, I wake up scared, like something happened, and I don’t feel rested at all.\n\nIt’s been messing with me. I used to be able to sleep after a shift if the room was dark enough—my neighbor’s dog used to bark in the mornings and I’d still sleep through it sometimes—but now it’s like even quiet doesn’t help. That’s why I came in, I think. I just feel like I’m not really sleeping anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood, like feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I do feel down. Most days it’s like I wake up already heavy, like there’s this empty feeling sitting on me before I even get out of bed.\n\nI used to go to church, and I used to do Zumba sometimes—my cousin kept telling me I had no rhythm, but I’d still go—and I helped with the kids at the center too. I don’t know… it feels like that version of me is just gone now. Like I open my eyes and she’s not there anymore.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how often you feel this way throughout the day? Does it seem to come and go, or is it pretty much there all the time?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s pretty much there most days. Like from the minute I open my eyes, I already feel heavy and empty, like I’m carrying something before anything even happens.\n\nSometimes I’ll think about how I used to get up for church, or go to Zumba even when I didn’t feel like it—there was this one lady who always stood in the front like she was on TV—and I used to help with the kids at the center too. Now it’s like that person is just gone. So, yeah… it doesn’t really come and go much.
